3. Add the API handlers

Put these before routes = [.

Python
Run
async def knowledge(request: Request):
    """
    Return the searchable cryptographic knowledge registry.

    Optional query parameters:
      ?q=RSA
      ?primitive=signature
      ?lifecycle=standardized
      ?quantum_posture=quantum_vulnerable
    """
    try:
        params = request.query_params

        query = (
            params.get("q", "")
            .strip()
            .lower()
        )

        primitive = (
            params.get("primitive", "")
            .strip()
            .lower()
        )

        lifecycle = (
            params.get("lifecycle", "")
            .strip()
            .lower()
        )

        quantum_posture = (
            params.get("quantum_posture", "")
            .strip()
            .lower()
        )

        records = []

        for algorithm in KNOWLEDGE.algorithms():
            searchable = " ".join(
                [
                    algorithm.name,
                    algorithm.family,
                    algorithm.description,
                    *algorithm.aliases,
                ]
            ).lower()

            if query and query not in searchable:
                continue

            if (
                primitive
                and algorithm.primitive.lower()
                != primitive
            ):
                continue

            if (
                lifecycle
                and algorithm.lifecycle_status.lower()
                != lifecycle
            ):
                continue

            if (
                quantum_posture
                and algorithm.quantum_posture.lower()
                != quantum_posture
            ):
                continue

            records.append(
                {
                    "knowledge_id": algorithm.knowledge_id,
                    "name": algorithm.name,
                    "aliases": list(
                        algorithm.aliases
                    ),
                    "family": algorithm.family,
                    "primitive": algorithm.primitive,
                    "purposes": list(
                        algorithm.purposes
                    ),
                    "lifecycle_status": (
                        algorithm.lifecycle_status
                    ),
                    "quantum_posture": (
                        algorithm.quantum_posture
                    ),
                    "security_strength": {
                        "classical_bits": (
                            algorithm.security_strength
                            .classical_bits
                        ),
                        "quantum_bits": (
                            algorithm.security_strength
                            .quantum_bits
                        ),
                        "basis": (
                            algorithm.security_strength
                            .basis
                        ),
                    },
                    "key_sizes": list(
                        algorithm.key_sizes
                    ),
                    "standards": list(
                        algorithm.standards
                    ),
                    "description": (
                        algorithm.description
                    ),
                    "notes": algorithm.notes,
                    "effective_from": (
                        algorithm.effective_from
                    ),
                    "effective_until": (
                        algorithm.effective_until
                    ),
                    "confidence": algorithm.confidence,
                }
            )

        return JSONResponse(
            {
                "projection": "knowledge",
                "knowledge_snapshot": (
                    KNOWLEDGE.snapshot()
                ),
                "records": records,
                "total": len(records),
            }
        )

    except Exception as exc:
        return JSONResponse(
            {
                "error": "Failed to load cryptographic knowledge.",
                "detail": str(exc),
            },
            status_code=500,
        )


async def knowledge_detail(request: Request):
    name = str(
        request.path_params.get(
            "name",
            "",
        )
    ).strip()

    if not name:
        return JSONResponse(
            {
                "error": "Algorithm name is required."
            },
            status_code=400,
        )

    result = KNOWLEDGE.resolve(
        name,
        purpose=request.query_params.get(
            "purpose"
        ),
        as_of=request.query_params.get(
            "as_of"
        ),
        target_type=request.query_params.get(
            "target_type"
        ),
        target_name=request.query_params.get(
            "target_name"
        ),
        target_version=request.query_params.get(
            "target_version"
        ),
    )

    if result.algorithm is None:
        return JSONResponse(
            {
                "error": "Cryptographic knowledge not found.",
                "query": name,
            },
            status_code=404,
        )

    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_detail",
            "knowledge_snapshot": (
                KNOWLEDGE.snapshot()
            ),
            "resolution": {
                "query": result.query,
                "normalized_query": (
                    result.normalized_query
                ),
                "matched_by": result.matched_by,
                "current": result.current,
                "algorithm": registry_algorithm_dict(
                    result.algorithm
                ),
                "standards": [
                    standard_dict(item)
                    for item in result.standards
                ],
                "compatibility": [
                    compatibility_dict(item)
                    for item in result.compatibility
                ],
                "migrations": [
                    migration_dict(item)
                    for item in result.migrations
                ],
                "conflicts": [
                    conflict_dict(item)
                    for item in result.conflicts
                ],
                "explainability": (
                    result.explainability
                ),
            },
        }
    )


async def knowledge_resolve(request: Request):
    try:
        payload = await request.json()
    except Exception:
        return JSONResponse(
            {
                "error": "Request body must be valid JSON."
            },
            status_code=400,
        )

    query = str(
        payload.get("query", "")
    ).strip()

    if not query:
        return JSONResponse(
            {
                "error": "query is required."
            },
            status_code=400,
        )

    result = KNOWLEDGE.resolve(
        query,
        purpose=payload.get("purpose"),
        as_of=payload.get("as_of"),
        target_type=payload.get(
            "target_type"
        ),
        target_name=payload.get(
            "target_name"
        ),
        target_version=payload.get(
            "target_version"
        ),
    )

    if result.algorithm is None:
        return JSONResponse(
            {
                "projection": "knowledge_resolution",
                "knowledge_snapshot": (
                    KNOWLEDGE.snapshot()
                ),
                "resolution": {
                    "status": "UNRESOLVED",
                    "query": query,
                    "explainability": (
                        result.explainability
                    ),
                },
            }
        )

    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_resolution",
            "knowledge_snapshot": (
                KNOWLEDGE.snapshot()
            ),
            "resolution": {
                "status": (
                    "CONFLICT"
                    if result.conflicts
                    else "RESOLVED"
                ),
                "query": result.query,
                "normalized_query": (
                    result.normalized_query
                ),
                "matched_by": result.matched_by,
                "current": result.current,
                "algorithm": registry_algorithm_dict(
                    result.algorithm
                ),
                "standards": [
                    standard_dict(item)
                    for item in result.standards
                ],
                "compatibility": [
                    compatibility_dict(item)
                    for item in result.compatibility
                ],
                "migrations": [
                    migration_dict(item)
                    for item in result.migrations
                ],
                "conflicts": [
                    conflict_dict(item)
                    for item in result.conflicts
                ],
                "explainability": (
                    result.explainability
                ),
            },
        }
    )


async def knowledge_snapshot(request: Request):
    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_snapshot",
            "snapshot": KNOWLEDGE.snapshot(),
        }
    )


async def knowledge_freshness(request: Request):
    try:
        max_age = int(
            request.query_params.get(
                "max_age_days",
                "180",
            )
        )
    except ValueError:
        return JSONResponse(
            {
                "error": (
                    "max_age_days must be an integer."
                )
            },
            status_code=400,
        )

    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_freshness",
            "knowledge_snapshot": (
                KNOWLEDGE.snapshot()
            ),
            "freshness": KNOWLEDGE.freshness(
                max_age_days=max_age,
                as_of=request.query_params.get(
                    "as_of"
                ),
            ),
        }
    )


async def knowledge_standards(request: Request):
    standards = [
        standard_dict(item)
        for item in KNOWLEDGE.standards()
    ]

    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_standards",
            "knowledge_snapshot": (
                KNOWLEDGE.snapshot()
            ),
            "standards": standards,
            "total": len(standards),
        }
    )


async def knowledge_migrations(request: Request):
    source = request.query_params.get(
        "source"
    )

    purpose = request.query_params.get(
        "purpose"
    )

    records = []

    for migration in KNOWLEDGE.migrations():
        if (
            source
            and migration.source_algorithm.lower()
            != source.lower()
        ):
            continue

        if (
            purpose
            and purpose.lower()
            not in {
                value.lower()
                for value in migration.applicable_purposes
            }
        ):
            continue

        records.append(
            migration_dict(migration)
        )

    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_migrations",
            "knowledge_snapshot": (
                KNOWLEDGE.snapshot()
            ),
            "migrations": records,
            "total": len(records),
        }
    )


async def knowledge_compatibility(request: Request):
    algorithm = request.query_params.get(
        "algorithm"
    )

    target_type = request.query_params.get(
        "target_type"
    )

    target_name = request.query_params.get(
        "target_name"
    )

    records = []

    for item in KNOWLEDGE.compatibility():
        if (
            algorithm
            and item.algorithm.lower()
            != algorithm.lower()
        ):
            continue

        if (
            target_type
            and item.target_type.lower()
            != target_type.lower()
        ):
            continue

        if (
            target_name
            and item.target_name.lower()
            != target_name.lower()
        ):
            continue

        records.append(
            compatibility_dict(item)
        )

    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_compatibility",
            "knowledge_snapshot": (
                KNOWLEDGE.snapshot()
            ),
            "compatibility": records,
            "total": len(records),
        }
    )

def registry_algorithm_dict(algorithm):
    return {
        "knowledge_id": algorithm.knowledge_id,
        "name": algorithm.name,
        "aliases": list(algorithm.aliases),
        "family": algorithm.family,
        "primitive": algorithm.primitive,
        "purposes": list(algorithm.purposes),
        "lifecycle_status": algorithm.lifecycle_status,
        "quantum_posture": algorithm.quantum_posture,
        "security_strength": {
            "classical_bits": (
                algorithm.security_strength.classical_bits
            ),
            "quantum_bits": (
                algorithm.security_strength.quantum_bits
            ),
            "basis": (
                algorithm.security_strength.basis
            ),
        },
        "key_sizes": list(algorithm.key_sizes),
        "standards": list(algorithm.standards),
        "description": algorithm.description,
        "notes": algorithm.notes,
        "effective_from": algorithm.effective_from,
        "effective_until": algorithm.effective_until,
        "source_ids": list(
            algorithm.source_ids
        ),
        "confidence": algorithm.confidence,
        "record_version": algorithm.record_version,
    }


def standard_dict(item):
    return {
        "standard_id": item.standard_id,
        "authority": item.authority,
        "identifier": item.identifier,
        "title": item.title,
        "status": item.status,
        "published_at": item.published_at,
        "effective_from": item.effective_from,
        "effective_until": item.effective_until,
        "related_algorithms": list(
            item.related_algorithms
        ),
        "supersedes": list(
            item.supersedes
        ),
        "source_ids": list(
            item.source_ids
        ),
        "confidence": item.confidence,
        "record_version": item.record_version,
    }


def migration_dict(item):
    return {
        "relationship_id": item.relationship_id,
        "source_algorithm": item.source_algorithm,
        "target_algorithm": item.target_algorithm,
        "relationship_type": item.relationship_type,
        "applicable_purposes": list(
            item.applicable_purposes
        ),
        "hybrid": item.hybrid,
        "prerequisites": list(
            item.prerequisites
        ),
        "constraints": list(
            item.constraints
        ),
        "source_ids": list(
            item.source_ids
        ),
        "effective_from": item.effective_from,
        "effective_until": item.effective_until,
        "confidence": item.confidence,
        "record_version": item.record_version,
    }


def compatibility_dict(item):
    return {
        "compatibility_id": item.compatibility_id,
        "algorithm": item.algorithm,
        "target_type": item.target_type,
        "target_name": item.target_name,
        "version_min": item.version_min,
        "version_max": item.version_max,
        "status": item.status,
        "constraints": list(
            item.constraints
        ),
        "source_ids": list(
            item.source_ids
        ),
        "effective_from": item.effective_from,
        "effective_until": item.effective_until,
        "confidence": item.confidence,
        "record_version": item.record_version,
    }


def conflict_dict(item):
    return {
        "conflict_id": item.conflict_id,
        "subject_type": item.subject_type,
        "subject_id": item.subject_id,
        "field": item.field,
        "values": list(item.values),
        "source_ids": list(item.source_ids),
        "resolution": item.resolution,
        "severity": item.severity,
    }
5. Add the routes

Inside your existing routes = [...], add these before /export/json:

Python
Run
    Route(
        "/knowledge",
        knowledge,
        methods=["GET"],
    ),
    Route(
        "/knowledge/snapshot",
        knowledge_snapshot,
        methods=["GET"],
    ),
    Route(
        "/knowledge/freshness",
        knowledge_freshness,
        methods=["GET"],
    ),
    Route(
        "/knowledge/standards",
        knowledge_standards,
        methods=["GET"],
    ),
    Route(
        "/knowledge/migrations",
        knowledge_migrations,
        methods=["GET"],
    ),
    Route(
        "/knowledge/compatibility",
        knowledge_compatibility,
        methods=["GET"],
    ),
    Route(
        "/knowledge/resolve",
        knowledge_resolve,
        methods=["POST"],
    ),
    Route(
        "/knowledge/{name:path}",
        knowledge_detail,
        methods=["GET"],
    ),

Order matters. Keep the explicit routes like /knowledge/resolve before {name:path}.

6. Add API tests

Create:

Bash
cd ~/ECDAT/ECDAT1

cat > backend/tests/test_knowledge_api.py <<'PY'
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import pytest
from starlette.testclient import TestClient

sys.path.insert(
    0,
    str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]),
)

from api import app


client = TestClient(app)


def test_knowledge_snapshot():
    response = client.get(
        "/knowledge/snapshot"
    )

    assert response.status_code == 200

    body = response.json()

    assert (
        body["snapshot"]["knowledge_version"]
        == "0.5.0"
    )

    assert len(
        body["snapshot"]["knowledge_hash"]
    ) == 64


def test_knowledge_search():
    response = client.get(
        "/knowledge?q=ML-KEM"
    )

    assert response.status_code == 200

    body = response.json()

    assert body["total"] >= 1

    names = {
        item["name"]
        for item in body["records"]
    }

    assert "ML-KEM" in names


def test_knowledge_detail():
    response = client.get(
        "/knowledge/ML-DSA"
    )

    assert response.status_code == 200

    body = response.json()

    algorithm = body["resolution"]["algorithm"]

    assert algorithm["name"] == "ML-DSA"
    assert algorithm["lifecycle_status"] == "standardized"


def test_unknown_knowledge():
    response = client.get(
        "/knowledge/DOES-NOT-EXIST"
    )

    assert response.status_code == 404


def test_resolve_rsa_signature():
    response = client.post(
        "/knowledge/resolve",
        json={
            "query": "RSA-2048",
            "purpose": "digital_signature",
        },
    )

    assert response.status_code == 200

    body = response.json()

    assert (
        body["resolution"]["status"]
        == "RESOLVED"
    )

    targets = {
        item["target_algorithm"]
        for item in body["resolution"]["migrations"]
    }

    assert "ML-DSA" in targets


def test_resolve_tls_hybrid():
    response = client.post(
        "/knowledge/resolve",
        json={
            "query": "X25519MLKEM768",
            "target_type": "protocol",
            "target_name": "TLS 1.3",
        },
    )

    assert response.status_code == 200

    body = response.json()

    assert (
        body["resolution"]["algorithm"]["name"]
        == "X25519MLKEM768"
    )

    compatibility = (
        body["resolution"]["compatibility"]
    )

    assert len(compatibility) == 1
    assert compatibility[0]["status"] == "supported"


def test_migrations_filter():
    response = client.get(
        "/knowledge/migrations",
        params={
            "source": "RSA",
            "purpose": "digital_signature",
        },
    )

    assert response.status_code == 200

    body = response.json()

    assert body["total"] >= 1

    assert all(
        item["source_algorithm"] == "RSA"
        for item in body["migrations"]
    )


def test_freshness_endpoint():
    response = client.get(
        "/knowledge/freshness",
        params={
            "as_of": "2026-09-05",
        },
    )

    assert response.status_code == 200

    body = response.json()

    assert (
        body["freshness"]["state"]
        == "fresh"
    )


def test_compatibility_endpoint():
    response = client.get(
        "/knowledge/compatibility",
        params={
            "algorithm": "X25519MLKEM768",
            "target_name": "TLS 1.3",
        },
    )

    assert response.status_code == 200

    body = response.json()

    assert body["total"] == 1

What this gives us
                         ECDAT API
                            │
             ┌──────────────┴──────────────┐
             │                             │
        Existing API                 Knowledge API
             │                             │
    scan/history/diff              search / resolve
    verify/export                  standards
                                   migrations
                                   compatibility
                                   freshness
                                   snapshot
             │                             │
             └──────────────┬──────────────┘
                            ▼
                    KnowledgeService
                            │
                    registry.json
                            │
                    SHA-256 snapshot

And importantly, the API doesn't flatten the RFC 10024 information. The three hybrid groups remain distinct knowledge records, which is correct because RFC 10024 gives them different traditional components and different IANA Recommended status. 
RFC Editor

Run the API tests first. If those are green, we then attach knowledge_snapshot to the actual ECDATScan / persisted scan state. That's the point where knowledge becomes part of the historical analytical record instead of being merely an auxiliary API.

Yes. You're right. We were slipping into a slow inspect → paste → inspect loop.

From here on, I'll give you one terminal batch at a time: paste it, run it, and it does the edits + tests + status in one shot. No manual file hunting unless absolutely necessary.

I also checked the current sources before this batch: NIST still identifies FIPS 203/204/205 as the principal finalized PQC standards, while additional algorithms remain in the standardization pipeline; RFC 10024 defines the three TLS 1.3 hybrid groups.

🚀 Batch: Knowledge API — direct terminal

from pathlib import Path

p = Path("backend/api.py")
s = p.read_text()

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 1. Imports
# ------------------------------------------------------------

anchor = """from analysis.verification import (
    build_verification,
    verification_to_dict,
)
"""

if "from knowledge.service import KnowledgeService" not in s:
    if anchor not in s:
        raise SystemExit("API verification import anchor not found")

    s = s.replace(
        anchor,
        anchor + """from knowledge.service import KnowledgeService
""",
        1,
    )

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 2. Knowledge service
# ------------------------------------------------------------

base_anchor = """BASE_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
"""

if "KNOWLEDGE = KnowledgeService()" not in s:
    if base_anchor not in s:
        raise SystemExit("BASE_DIR anchor not found")

    s = s.replace(
        base_anchor,
        base_anchor + """
KNOWLEDGE = KnowledgeService()
""",
        1,
    )

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 3. Serialization helpers + handlers
# ------------------------------------------------------------

if "async def knowledge(request: Request):" not in s:

    marker = "\nroutes = [\n"

    if marker not in s:
        raise SystemExit("routes marker not found")

    block = r'''

def _knowledge_algorithm_dict(item):
    return {
        "knowledge_id": item.knowledge_id,
        "name": item.name,
        "aliases": list(item.aliases),
        "family": item.family,
        "primitive": item.primitive,
        "purposes": list(item.purposes),
        "lifecycle_status": item.lifecycle_status,
        "quantum_posture": item.quantum_posture,
        "security_strength": {
            "classical_bits": item.security_strength.classical_bits,
            "quantum_bits": item.security_strength.quantum_bits,
            "basis": item.security_strength.basis,
        },
        "key_sizes": list(item.key_sizes),
        "standards": list(item.standards),
        "description": item.description,
        "notes": item.notes,
        "effective_from": item.effective_from,
        "effective_until": item.effective_until,
        "source_ids": list(item.source_ids),
        "confidence": item.confidence,
        "record_version": item.record_version,
    }


def _knowledge_standard_dict(item):
    return {
        "standard_id": item.standard_id,
        "authority": item.authority,
        "identifier": item.identifier,
        "title": item.title,
        "status": item.status,
        "published_at": item.published_at,
        "effective_from": item.effective_from,
        "effective_until": item.effective_until,
        "related_algorithms": list(item.related_algorithms),
        "supersedes": list(item.supersedes),
        "source_ids": list(item.source_ids),
        "confidence": item.confidence,
        "record_version": item.record_version,
    }


def _knowledge_migration_dict(item):
    return {
        "relationship_id": item.relationship_id,
        "source_algorithm": item.source_algorithm,
        "target_algorithm": item.target_algorithm,
        "relationship_type": item.relationship_type,
        "applicable_purposes": list(item.applicable_purposes),
        "hybrid": item.hybrid,
        "prerequisites": list(item.prerequisites),
        "constraints": list(item.constraints),
        "source_ids": list(item.source_ids),
        "effective_from": item.effective_from,
        "effective_until": item.effective_until,
        "confidence": item.confidence,
        "record_version": item.record_version,
    }


def _knowledge_compatibility_dict(item):
    return {
        "compatibility_id": item.compatibility_id,
        "algorithm": item.algorithm,
        "target_type": item.target_type,
        "target_name": item.target_name,
        "version_min": item.version_min,
        "version_max": item.version_max,
        "status": item.status,
        "constraints": list(item.constraints),
        "source_ids": list(item.source_ids),
        "effective_from": item.effective_from,
        "effective_until": item.effective_until,
        "confidence": item.confidence,
        "record_version": item.record_version,
    }


def _knowledge_conflict_dict(item):
    return {
        "conflict_id": item.conflict_id,
        "subject_type": item.subject_type,
        "subject_id": item.subject_id,
        "field": item.field,
        "values": list(item.values),
        "source_ids": list(item.source_ids),
        "resolution": item.resolution,
        "severity": item.severity,
    }


async def knowledge(request: Request):
    params = request.query_params

    query = params.get("q", "").strip().lower()
    primitive = params.get("primitive", "").strip().lower()
    lifecycle = params.get("lifecycle", "").strip().lower()
    quantum = params.get("quantum_posture", "").strip().lower()

    records = []

    for item in KNOWLEDGE.algorithms():
        searchable = " ".join(
            [
                item.name,
                item.family,
                item.description,
                *item.aliases,
            ]
        ).lower()

        if query and query not in searchable:
            continue

        if primitive and item.primitive.lower() != primitive:
            continue

        if lifecycle and item.lifecycle_status.lower() != lifecycle:
            continue

        if quantum and item.quantum_posture.lower() != quantum:
            continue

        records.append(
            _knowledge_algorithm_dict(item)
        )

    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge",
            "knowledge_snapshot": KNOWLEDGE.snapshot(),
            "records": records,
            "total": len(records),
        }
    )


async def knowledge_snapshot(request: Request):
    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_snapshot",
            "snapshot": KNOWLEDGE.snapshot(),
        }
    )


async def knowledge_freshness(request: Request):
    raw_max_age = request.query_params.get(
        "max_age_days",
        "180",
    )

    try:
        max_age = int(raw_max_age)
    except ValueError:
        return JSONResponse(
            {
                "error": "max_age_days must be an integer."
            },
            status_code=400,
        )

    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_freshness",
            "knowledge_snapshot": KNOWLEDGE.snapshot(),
            "freshness": KNOWLEDGE.freshness(
                max_age_days=max_age,
                as_of=request.query_params.get("as_of"),
            ),
        }
    )


async def knowledge_standards(request: Request):
    records = [
        _knowledge_standard_dict(item)
        for item in KNOWLEDGE.standards()
    ]

    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_standards",
            "knowledge_snapshot": KNOWLEDGE.snapshot(),
            "standards": records,
            "total": len(records),
        }
    )


async def knowledge_migrations(request: Request):
    source = request.query_params.get("source")
    purpose = request.query_params.get("purpose")

    records = []

    for item in KNOWLEDGE.migrations():

        if source and item.source_algorithm.lower() != source.lower():
            continue

        if purpose and purpose.lower() not in {
            value.lower()
            for value in item.applicable_purposes
        }:
            continue

        records.append(
            _knowledge_migration_dict(item)
        )

    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_migrations",
            "knowledge_snapshot": KNOWLEDGE.snapshot(),
            "migrations": records,
            "total": len(records),
        }
    )


async def knowledge_compatibility(request: Request):
    algorithm = request.query_params.get("algorithm")
    target_type = request.query_params.get("target_type")
    target_name = request.query_params.get("target_name")

    records = []

    for item in KNOWLEDGE.compatibility():

        if algorithm and item.algorithm.lower() != algorithm.lower():
            continue

        if target_type and item.target_type.lower() != target_type.lower():
            continue

        if target_name and item.target_name.lower() != target_name.lower():
            continue

        records.append(
            _knowledge_compatibility_dict(item)
        )

    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_compatibility",
            "knowledge_snapshot": KNOWLEDGE.snapshot(),
            "compatibility": records,
            "total": len(records),
        }
    )


async def knowledge_detail(request: Request):
    name = request.path_params.get("name", "").strip()

    if not name:
        return JSONResponse(
            {
                "error": "Algorithm name is required."
            },
            status_code=400,
        )

    result = KNOWLEDGE.resolve(
        name,
        purpose=request.query_params.get("purpose"),
        as_of=request.query_params.get("as_of"),
        target_type=request.query_params.get("target_type"),
        target_name=request.query_params.get("target_name"),
        target_version=request.query_params.get("target_version"),
    )

    if result.algorithm is None:
        return JSONResponse(
            {
                "error": "Cryptographic knowledge not found.",
                "query": name,
                "explainability": result.explainability,
            },
            status_code=404,
        )

    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_detail",
            "knowledge_snapshot": KNOWLEDGE.snapshot(),
            "resolution": {
                "query": result.query,
                "normalized_query": result.normalized_query,
                "matched_by": result.matched_by,
                "current": result.current,
                "algorithm": _knowledge_algorithm_dict(
                    result.algorithm
                ),
                "standards": [
                    _knowledge_standard_dict(item)
                    for item in result.standards
                ],
                "compatibility": [
                    _knowledge_compatibility_dict(item)
                    for item in result.compatibility
                ],
                "migrations": [
                    _knowledge_migration_dict(item)
                    for item in result.migrations
                ],
                "conflicts": [
                    _knowledge_conflict_dict(item)
                    for item in result.conflicts
                ],
                "explainability": result.explainability,
            },
        }
    )


async def knowledge_resolve(request: Request):
    try:
        payload = await request.json()
    except Exception:
        return JSONResponse(
            {
                "error": "Request body must be valid JSON."
            },
            status_code=400,
        )

    query = str(
        payload.get("query", "")
    ).strip()

    if not query:
        return JSONResponse(
            {
                "error": "query is required."
            },
            status_code=400,
        )

    result = KNOWLEDGE.resolve(
        query,
        purpose=payload.get("purpose"),
        as_of=payload.get("as_of"),
        target_type=payload.get("target_type"),
        target_name=payload.get("target_name"),
        target_version=payload.get("target_version"),
    )

    return JSONResponse(
        {
            "projection": "knowledge_resolution",
            "knowledge_snapshot": KNOWLEDGE.snapshot(),
            "resolution": {
                "status": (
                    "UNRESOLVED"
                    if result.algorithm is None
                    else (
                        "CONFLICT"
                        if result.conflicts
                        else "RESOLVED"
                    )
                ),
                "query": result.query,
                "normalized_query": result.normalized_query,
                "matched_by": result.matched_by,
                "current": result.current,
                "algorithm": (
                    None
                    if result.algorithm is None
                    else _knowledge_algorithm_dict(
                        result.algorithm
                    )
                ),
                "standards": [
                    _knowledge_standard_dict(item)
                    for item in result.standards
                ],
                "compatibility": [
                    _knowledge_compatibility_dict(item)
                    for item in result.compatibility
                ],
                "migrations": [
                    _knowledge_migration_dict(item)
                    for item in result.migrations
                ],
                "conflicts": [
                    _knowledge_conflict_dict(item)
                    for item in result.conflicts
                ],
                "explainability": result.explainability,
            },
        }
    )
'''

    s = s.replace(
        marker,
        block + marker,
        1,
    )

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 4. Routes

route_anchor = """    Route(
        "/export/json",
        export_json,
        methods=["POST"],
    ),
"""

if '"/knowledge"' not in s:
    if route_anchor not in s:
        raise SystemExit("export route anchor not found")

    routes = """    Route(
        "/knowledge",
        knowledge,
        methods=["GET"],
    ),

        "/knowledge/snapshot",
        knowledge_snapshot,

"""

    )

What we do next

Don't add more algorithm records.

Instead, extend the existing AlgorithmKnowledge model so those existing records can express:

alg:x25519mlkem768
    family: hybrid
    primitive: key_agreement
    components:
        alg:x25519
        alg:ml-kem
    protocol:
        TLS 1.3
    IANA:
        recommended = true

and similarly:

alg:secp256r1mlkem768
    → alg:secp256r1 + alg:ml-kem

alg:secp384r1mlkem1024
    → alg:secp384r1 + alg:ml-kem

That is substantially better than adding arbitrary fields like PQC_HYBRID and pretending they're already part of ECDAT's model.

cat > backend/tests/test_knowledge_hybrids.py <<'PY'
from dataclasses import replace

import pytest

from knowledge.registry import get_registry
from knowledge.validation import validate_registry


HYBRID_IDS = (
    "alg:x25519mlkem768",
    "alg:secp256r1mlkem768",
    "alg:secp384r1mlkem1024",
)


def _by_id():
    return {
        item.knowledge_id: item
        for item in get_registry().algorithms
    }


def _registry_with_algorithm(replacement):
    registry = get_registry()
    algorithms = tuple(
        replacement if item.knowledge_id == replacement.knowledge_id else item
        for item in registry.algorithms
    )
    return replace(registry, algorithms=algorithms)


def test_rfc_10024_hybrids_have_expected_components():
    algorithms = _by_id()

    assert algorithms["alg:x25519mlkem768"].components == (
        "alg:x25519",
        "alg:ml-kem",
    )
    assert algorithms["alg:x25519mlkem768"].parameters == ("ML-KEM-768",)

    assert algorithms["alg:secp256r1mlkem768"].components == (
        "alg:secp256r1",
        "alg:ml-kem",
    )
    assert algorithms["alg:secp256r1mlkem768"].parameters == ("ML-KEM-768",)

    assert algorithms["alg:secp384r1mlkem1024"].components == (
        "alg:secp384r1",
        "alg:ml-kem",
    )
    assert algorithms["alg:secp384r1mlkem1024"].parameters == (
        "ML-KEM-1024",
    )


def test_hybrid_components_resolve():
    algorithms = _by_id()

    for hybrid_id in HYBRID_IDS:
        hybrid = algorithms[hybrid_id]

        assert hybrid.primitive == "composite"
        assert hybrid.components

        for component_id in hybrid.components:
            assert component_id in algorithms


def test_hybrid_parameters_belong_to_declared_components():
    algorithms = _by_id()

    for hybrid_id in HYBRID_IDS:
        hybrid = algorithms[hybrid_id]
        components = [
            algorithms[component_id]
            for component_id in hybrid.components
        ]

        for parameter in hybrid.parameters:
            assert any(
                parameter in component.parameters
                for component in components
            )


def test_registry_rejects_unknown_hybrid_component():
    algorithms = _by_id()
    hybrid = algorithms["alg:x25519mlkem768"]

    broken_hybrid = replace(
        hybrid,
        components=(
            "alg:x25519",
            "alg:does-not-exist",
        ),
    )

    broken_registry = _registry_with_algorithm(broken_hybrid)

    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="unknown component"):
        validate_registry(broken_registry)


def test_registry_rejects_unknown_hybrid_parameter():
    algorithms = _by_id()
    hybrid = algorithms["alg:x25519mlkem768"]

    broken_hybrid = replace(
        hybrid,
        parameters=("ML-KEM-999",),
    )

    broken_registry = _registry_with_algorithm(broken_hybrid)

    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="unknown component parameter"):
        validate_registry(broken_registry)

from pathlib import Path

path = Path("backend/model/schema.py")
s = path.read_text()

# Insert KnowledgeSnapshot immediately before ECDATScan.
needle = "@dataclass\nclass ECDATScan:\n"

replacement = '''@dataclass
class KnowledgeSnapshot:
    """
    Immutable knowledge context used to produce an ECDAT analysis.

    The version and integrity hash identify the exact knowledge
    registry state used by the analysis.
    """

    knowledge_version: str
    knowledge_hash: str
    generated_at: Optional[str] = None


@dataclass
class ECDATScan:
'''

if s.count(needle) != 1:
    raise SystemExit(
        f"ECDATScan anchor expected once, found {s.count(needle)}"
    )

s = s.replace(needle, replacement, 1)

# Add snapshot as the first analytical context on ECDATScan.
needle = '''class ECDATScan:
    metadata: ScanMetadata
    summary: ScanSummary

    applications: List[Application] = field(default_factory=list)
'''

replacement = '''class ECDATScan:
    metadata: ScanMetadata
    summary: ScanSummary

    knowledge_snapshot: Optional[KnowledgeSnapshot] = None

    applications: List[Application] = field(default_factory=list)

from pathlib import Path

path = Path("backend/analysis/report_builder.py")
s = path.read_text()

# Add KnowledgeService import.
if "from knowledge.service import KnowledgeService" not in s:
    lines = s.splitlines()
    insert_at = 0

    while insert_at < len(lines) and (
        lines[insert_at].startswith("from ")
        or lines[insert_at].startswith("import ")
        or not lines[insert_at].strip()
    ):
        insert_at += 1

    lines.insert(insert_at, "from knowledge.service import KnowledgeService")
    s = "\n".join(lines) + "\n"

# Add service creation and snapshot to build_report.
needle = '''    canonical_scan = build_canonical_scan(
        scan_results,
        mosca_inputs=mosca_inputs,
    )
'''

replacement = '''    knowledge_service = KnowledgeService()

    canonical_scan = build_canonical_scan(
        scan_results,
        mosca_inputs=mosca_inputs,
    )

    knowledge_snapshot = knowledge_service.snapshot()
'''

if s.count(needle) != 1:
    raise SystemExit(
        f"build_report canonical_scan anchor expected once, found {s.count(needle)}"
    )

s = s.replace(needle, replacement, 1)

# Find the final report return and inject the snapshot.
needle = '''        "risk_landscape": risk_landscape,
    }
'''

if s.count(needle) != 1:
    raise SystemExit(
        f"report return anchor expected once, found {s.count(needle)}"
    )

replacement = '''        "risk_landscape": risk_landscape,
        "knowledge_snapshot": knowledge_snapshot,
    }
'''

s = s.replace(needle, replacement, 1)

path.write_text(s)
print("Connected KnowledgeService snapshot to build_report().")

from knowledge.service import KnowledgeService
from analysis.report_builder import build_report


def test_knowledge_service_snapshot_has_identity():
    snapshot = KnowledgeService().snapshot()

    assert snapshot["knowledge_version"]
    assert snapshot["knowledge_hash"]
    assert snapshot["generated_at"]


def test_report_contains_knowledge_snapshot():
    report = build_report(
        {
            "artifacts": [],
            "applications": [],
            "evidence": [],
        }
    )

    snapshot = report["knowledge_snapshot"]

    assert snapshot["knowledge_version"] == KnowledgeService().version
    assert snapshot["knowledge_hash"] == KnowledgeService().integrity_hash
    assert snapshot["generated_at"] == (
        KnowledgeService().registry.manifest.generated_at
    )


def test_scan_state_preserves_knowledge_snapshot():
    from model.scan_state_builder import build_scan_state

    report = build_report(
        {
            "artifacts": [],
            "applications": [],
            "evidence": [],
        }
    )

    state = build_scan_state(report)

    assert state.knowledge_snapshot == report["knowledge_snapshot"]

We're looking specifically for whether the recommendation's existing knowledge_version / knowledge_hash are merely duplicated scalar fields or whether we can cleanly expose the same snapshot object without breaking the existing API.

The target architecture is:

Scan
 └── knowledge_snapshot
       ├── version
       ├── hash
       └── generated_at

Recommendation
 └── knowledge_snapshot
       ├── version
       ├── hash
       └── generated_at

Then invalidation becomes deterministic:

same snapshot → CURRENT

different version/hash → STALE → re-analysis required

missing/malformed → UNKNOWN

And we preserve the existing scalar fields for backward compatibility rather than breaking the frontend/API.

from pathlib import Path

p = Path("backend/model/canonical.py")
s = p.read_text()

s = s.replace(
    "from analysis.recommendations import get_recommendation",
    "from analysis.recommendations import (\n"
    "    build_recommendation as build_knowledge_recommendation,\n"
    "    get_migration_candidates,\n"
    ")",
)

start = s.index("def classify_recommendation(")
end = s.index("\ndef build_canonical_artifact(", start)

replacement = '''def build_recommendation(
    artifact: Dict[str, Any],
) -> Recommendation:
    """
    Project the knowledge-driven recommendation into the canonical model.

    Cryptographic decision logic belongs to analysis/knowledge. The
    canonical layer stores the resulting decision and its provenance.
    """
    result = build_knowledge_recommendation(artifact)

    category = "manual_review"
    status = str(result.get("status", "UNRESOLVED")).upper()

    if status == "RESOLVED":
        candidates = result.get("candidates") or []

        if candidates:
            category = "migrate"
        elif str(result.get("quantum_posture", "")) == "quantum_resistant":
            category = "monitor"
        else:
            category = "manual_review"

    elif status == "CONFLICT":
        category = "manual_review"

    return Recommendation(
        recommendation_id=stable_id(
            "recommendation",
            artifact.get("artifact_id"),
            category,
        ),
        category=category,
        priority=str(
            result.get(
                "priority",
                "REVIEW",
            )
        ).lower(),
        text=str(
            result.get(
                "text",
                "Perform manual cryptographic review.",
            )
        ),
        rationale=str(
            result.get(
                "rationale",
                "Further cryptographic review is required.",
            )
        ),
    )


def build_migration_options(
    artifact: Dict[str, Any],
    recommendation: Recommendation,
) -> List[MigrationOption]:
    """
    Project knowledge-derived migration relationships into the
    canonical migration-option model.

    No cryptographic algorithm-specific migration rules belong here.
    """
    candidates = get_migration_candidates(artifact)

    options: List[MigrationOption] = []

    for candidate in candidates:
        target = str(
            candidate.get(
                "target_algorithm",
                "",
            )
        )

        relationship_type = str(
            candidate.get(
                "relationship_type",
                "alternative_to",
            )
        )

        hybrid = bool(
            candidate.get(
                "hybrid",
                False,
            )
        )

        confidence = str(
            candidate.get(
                "confidence",
                "unknown",
            )
        )

        prerequisites = candidate.get(
            "prerequisites"
        ) or []

        constraints = candidate.get(
            "constraints"
        ) or []

        rationale = (
            f"Knowledge-linked {relationship_type} candidate "
            f"for {candidate.get('source_algorithm', artifact.get('algorithm'))} "
            f"→ {target}."
        )

        if hybrid:
            rationale += " This is a hybrid migration mechanism."

        compatibility = (
            "Requires compatibility assessment."
        )

        if constraints:
            compatibility = (
                "Constraints: "
                + "; ".join(
                    str(item)
                    for item in constraints
                )
                + "."
            )

        if prerequisites:
            compatibility += (
                " Prerequisites: "
                + "; ".join(
                    str(item)
                    for item in prerequisites
                )
                + "."
            )

        effort = {
            "high": "High",
            "medium": "Medium",
            "low": "Low",
        }.get(
            confidence.lower(),
            "Medium",
        )

        options.append(
            MigrationOption(
                option_id=stable_id(
                    "migration",
                    artifact.get("artifact_id"),
                    candidate.get("relationship_id"),
                ),
                name=target,
                rationale=rationale,
                compatibility=compatibility,
                effort=effort,
            )
        )

    return options
'''

s = s[:start] + replacement + s[end:]

p.write_text(s)

Correct repair

We need to restore the helper inside canonical.py, exactly where it belongs.

Run only this:

Bash
cd ~/ECDAT/ECDAT1

python - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path

p = Path("backend/model/canonical.py")
s = p.read_text()

# Remove the incorrect identity import.
s = s.replace(
"""from model.identity import (
    build_application_id,
    build_artifact_id,
    build_canonical_artifact_id,
    build_evidence_id,
    build_scan_id,
    build_verification_id,
)""",
"""from model.identity import (
    build_artifact_id,
    build_evidence_id,
)"""
)

# If the canonical helper was removed by the refactor, restore it.
if "def build_canonical_artifact_id(" not in s:
    marker = "\ndef build_canonical_artifact(\n"

    helper = '''
def build_canonical_artifact_id(
    artifact: Dict[str, Any],
    application_id: str,
) -> str:
    """
    Build stable logical identity for a cryptographic artifact.

    Source file, line number, scan timestamp, and scanner
    discovery order are deliberately excluded.

    Multiple indistinguishable source observations represent
    one logical artifact and are modeled through evidence.
    """
    algorithm = str(
        artifact.get(
            "algorithm",
            "UNKNOWN",
        )
    )

    artifact_type = str(
        artifact.get(
            "type",
            "cryptographic_artifact",
        )
    )

    purpose = str(
        artifact.get(
            "purpose",
            "unknown",
        )
    )

    details = dict(
        artifact.get(
            "details",
            {},
        )
    )

    for key in (
        "key_size",
        "mode",
        "curve",
        "version",
        "algorithm_family",
    ):
        value = artifact.get(key)

        if value is not None:
            details[key] = value

    semantic_signature = artifact.get(
        "semantic_signature"
    )

    return build_artifact_id(
        application_id=application_id,
        algorithm=algorithm,
        artifact_type=artifact_type,
        purpose=purpose,
        details=details,
        semantic_signature=semantic_signature,
    )

'''

    if marker not in s:
        raise RuntimeError(
            "Could not locate build_canonical_artifact(). "
            "Refusing to modify the file."
        )

    s = s.replace(marker, "\n" + helper + marker, 1)

p.write_text(s)
PY

Run:

Bash
cd ~/ECDAT/ECDAT1

python - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path

p = Path("backend/model/canonical.py")
s = p.read_text()

marker = "from model.schema import (\n"

if marker not in s:
    insert_at = s.index("\n\ndef stable_id(")

    imports = """
from model.relationships import (
    build_components,
    build_relationships,
    map_components_to_artifacts,
)
from model.schema import (
    Algorithm,
    Application,
    BusinessContext,
    CryptoArtifact,
    Detection,
    ECDATScan,
    Evidence,
    MigrationOption,
    MoscaAssessment,
    Purpose,
    Recommendation,
    Risk,
    RiskAssessment,
    ScanMetadata,
    ScanSummary,
    VerificationState,
)
"""

    s = s[:insert_at] + imports + s[insert_at:]
    p.write_text(s)
else:
    print("Schema imports already present; refusing to modify.")
PY

Run:

Bash
cd ~/ECDAT/ECDAT1

python - <<'PY'
import subprocess
from pathlib import Path

p = Path("backend/model/canonical.py")
local = p.read_text()

baseline = subprocess.check_output(
    ["git", "show", "feature/crypto-knowledge:backend/model/canonical.py"],
    text=True,
)

start = baseline.index("def build_verification_state(")
end = baseline.index("\ndef build_canonical_scan(", start)

function = baseline[start:end]

if "def build_verification_state(" in local:
    print("build_verification_state already exists; refusing to modify.")
else:
    marker = "\ndef build_canonical_scan("
    if marker not in local:
        raise RuntimeError("Could not locate build_canonical_scan()")
    local = local.replace(marker, "\n" + function + marker, 1)
    p.write_text(local)
    print("Restored build_verification_state() from Git baseline.")

Perfect. Next step: lock the provenance contract with a test, then do the final regression/diff review.

Run this single batch:

Bash
cd ~/ECDAT/ECDAT1

cat > backend/tests/test_canonical_recommendation_provenance.py <<'PY'
from analysis.report_builder import build_report
from model.canonical import build_canonical_scan
from knowledge.service import KnowledgeService


def _artifact():
    return {
        "artifact_id": "artifact:test-provenance",
        "algorithm": "RSA",
        "type": "cryptographic_artifact",
        "purpose": "signature",
        "risk": {
            "quantum": {
                "level": "HIGH",
            }
        },
        "details": {},
    }


def test_canonical_recommendation_preserves_knowledge_provenance():
    service = KnowledgeService()

    artifact = _artifact()

    result = build_canonical_scan(
        {
            "target": "provenance-test",
            "total_files_scanned": 1,
            "artifacts": [artifact],
            "risk_summary": {},
            "quantum_vulnerable_assets": 1,
        }
    )

    assert result.recommendations

    recommendation = result.recommendations[0]

    assert recommendation.knowledge_version == service.version
    assert recommendation.knowledge_hash == service.integrity_hash
    assert recommendation.status in {
        "RESOLVED",
        "CONFLICT",
        "UNRESOLVED",
    }

    assert isinstance(recommendation.candidate_ids, list)
    assert isinstance(recommendation.candidates, list)

    if recommendation.candidates:
        candidate = recommendation.candidates[0]

        assert candidate["relationship_id"]
        assert candidate["target_algorithm"]
        assert "relationship_type" in candidate
        assert "hybrid" in candidate


def test_report_preserves_recommendation_and_migration_provenance():
    service = KnowledgeService()

    report = build_report(
        {
            "target": "provenance-test",
            "total_files_scanned": 1,
            "artifacts": [_artifact()],
            "risk_summary": {},
            "quantum_vulnerable_assets": 1,
        }
    )

    assert report["knowledge_snapshot"]["knowledge_version"] == service.version
    assert report["knowledge_snapshot"]["knowledge_hash"] == service.integrity_hash

    recommendations = report["recommendations"]
    assert recommendations

    recommendation = recommendations[0]

    assert recommendation["knowledge_version"] == service.version
    assert recommendation["knowledge_hash"] == service.integrity_hash
    assert "candidate_ids" in recommendation
    assert "candidates" in recommendation
    assert "explainability" in recommendation

    for option in report["migration_options"]:
        assert option["knowledge_version"] == service.version
        assert option["knowledge_hash"] == service.integrity_hash
        assert option["relationship_id"]
        assert option["target_algorithm"]
PY

printf '\n===== PROVENANCE TESTS =====\n'
PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1 pytest -q \
  backend/tests/test_canonical_recommendation_provenance.py

The remaining unchecked/partial items are mostly the next maturity layer, not missing fundamentals.

What I'd prioritize next

P0 — finish the knowledge engine properly

Protocol applicability

Library/provider compatibility

Realistic fixtures

P2 — API maturity

Search/filter/pagination

API error contracts

And one thing I'd specifically not do yet: build a giant fancy UI for these. The knowledge layer should become structurally excellent first; then the UI can expose it.

One architectural gap worth fixing

The biggest genuine hole is:

Knowledge source update mechanism

Right now we have a versioned, hashed, validated registry. That's good.

But enterprise ECDAT eventually needs:

Authoritative sources
       ↓
Ingestion/update
       ↓
Schema validation
       ↓
Conflict detection
       ↓
Authority resolution
       ↓
New registry version
       ↓
Integrity hash
       ↓
Impact analysis
       ↓
Recommendation invalidation

That's what turns the registry from a static knowledge database into a living cryptographic intelligence system.

So I would mark the checklist approximately:

Core knowledge architecture: ~90% complete.

Full production knowledge lifecycle: ~65–70%.

And importantly, we should not reopen the already-completed architecture just because some advanced boxes remain unchecked. The current commit is a legitimate checkpoint.

Recommendation

      ↓
MigrationOption
 ├── knowledge_version
 └── knowledge_hash

And the provenance integration tests pass.

One nuance

ECDATScan itself currently does not populate its knowledge_snapshot field:

Python
Run
return ECDATScan(
    ...
    verification=verification_states,
)

There is no:

Python
Run
knowledge_snapshot=...

So the architecture is currently:

ECDATScan
 ├── recommendations → knowledge provenance ✅
 ├── migration options → knowledge provenance ✅
 └── knowledge_snapshot → None ⚠️

Report
 └── knowledge_snapshot → persisted ✅

ScanState
 └── knowledge_snapshot → persisted from report ✅

That's not a blocker for the current system because the report/history path is the persisted analytical snapshot, and your tests protect it.

But architecturally, since ECDATScan already has the field, I would eventually make the canonical model self-contained too. I would not make that change right now, because we've already validated the current contract and it would create another unnecessary modification before pushing.

Sources

Good. This baseline tells us something important: v0.5 is already much further along than the checklist initially suggested. The missing work is now mostly about making the knowledge system more expressive and lifecycle-safe, not rebuilding it.

The current model already has explicit compatibility constraints, migration relationships, temporal validity, provenance, lifecycle, conflicts, confidence, security strength, aliases, primitives, purposes, and registry hashing. 

Pasted text

For example, RFC 10024 gives us authoritative protocol metadata for the three TLS 1.3 PQ/T hybrids we already model, including DTLS applicability and IANA "Recommended" status. 
RFC Editor
 NIST FIPS 203 similarly gives authoritative ML-KEM parameter-set information. 
NIST Computer Security Resource Center

Pass 1: make applicability genuinely enterprise-grade

I want to add these without breaking the existing registry format:

1. Protocol applicability

Add structured metadata for things such as:

protocol
protocol_version
usage
status
recommended
dtls_ok
source_ids

This lets ECDAT distinguish:

X25519MLKEM768
  TLS 1.3 → supported / recommended
  DTLS    → supported

from merely saying "this is a hybrid."

2. Provider/library compatibility

Extend compatibility records so they can express:

algorithm
target_type = library | provider | implementation
target_name
version_min
version_max
status
constraints

Your existing model already has most of this. We're going to make it actually useful rather than inventing another subsystem.

3. Version constraints

Your version_matches() already exists. We'll harden it so versions like:

3.0
3.0.1
3.0.1-beta

don't get treated as primitive numeric strings incorrectly.

4. Interoperability constraints

Represent constraints explicitly instead of burying them in free-text notes.

5. Standard supersession

You already have:

Python
Run
supersedes: Tuple[str, ...]

but we need validation and resolution semantics around it.

6. Authority ranking

This is important for conflict handling.

Something like:

NIST / IETF / ISO
       ↓
official standard
       ↓
official implementation/provider documentation
       ↓
secondary source

But we will not invent arbitrary rankings. The ranking will be registry metadata/policy, not a cryptographic fact.

One more important thing

Your current registry loader calculates the hash from the raw JSON, which is good for integrity, but the manifest doesn't currently include a schema-evolution policy beyond:

Python
Run
SCHEMA_VERSION = "1.0.0"

We'll handle schema evolution later in the lifecycle pass.

And your current registry has:

conflicts: 0

because conflicts aren't actually being computed during load_registry().

That's a real v0.5 gap.

Currently:

load_registry()
     ↓
parse
     ↓
validate
     ↓
conflicts = ()

Eventually we want:

load raw
   ↓
parse
   ↓
validate
   ↓
detect conflicts
   ↓
apply conservative conflict policy
   ↓
build registry

That's Pass 2, not Pass 1.

Let's implement Pass 1

I'm deliberately keeping this to one coherent change.

import json
from pathlib import Path

p = Path("backend/knowledge/data/registry.json")
data = json.loads(p.read_text())

protocols = data.setdefault("protocols", [])

existing = {
    item["applicability_id"]
    for item in protocols
}

records = [
    {
        "applicability_id": "protocol:x25519mlkem768:tls13",
        "algorithm": "X25519MLKEM768",
        "protocol": "TLS",
        "protocol_version": "1.3",
        "usage": "key_agreement",
        "status": "supported",
        "recommended": True,
        "dtls_ok": True,
        "constraints": [
            "Requires TLS 1.3 supported-groups negotiation.",
            "Combines X25519 with ML-KEM-768.",
        ],
        "source_ids": ["ietf:rfc-10024"],
        "effective_from": "2026-01-01",
        "effective_until": None,
        "confidence": "high",
        "record_version": 1,
    },
    {
        "applicability_id": "protocol:secp256r1mlkem768:tls13",
        "algorithm": "SecP256r1MLKEM768",
        "protocol": "TLS",
        "protocol_version": "1.3",
        "usage": "key_agreement",
        "status": "supported",
        "recommended": False,
        "dtls_ok": True,
        "constraints": [
            "Requires TLS 1.3 supported-groups negotiation.",
            "Combines secp256r1 ECDH with ML-KEM-768.",
        ],
        "source_ids": ["ietf:rfc-10024"],
        "effective_from": "2026-01-01",
        "effective_until": None,
        "confidence": "high",
        "record_version": 1,
    },
    {
        "applicability_id": "protocol:secp384r1mlkem1024:tls13",
        "algorithm": "SecP384r1MLKEM1024",
        "protocol": "TLS",
        "protocol_version": "1.3",
        "usage": "key_agreement",
        "status": "supported",
        "recommended": False,
        "dtls_ok": True,
        "constraints": [
            "Requires TLS 1.3 supported-groups negotiation.",
            "Combines secp384r1 ECDH with ML-KEM-1024.",
        ],
        "source_ids": ["ietf:rfc-10024"],
        "effective_from": "2026-01-01",
        "effective_until": None,
        "confidence": "high",
        "record_version": 1,
    },
]

for record in records:
    if record["applicability_id"] not in existing:
        protocols.append(record)

data["protocols"] = protocols

p.write_text(
    json.dumps(
        data,
        indent=2,
        ensure_ascii=True,
    ) + "\n"

Pass 2 — Authority + conflicts + supersession

This is the next piece that makes the registry behave like an actual knowledge system, rather than a JSON file with crypto facts.

Right now there is a weakness:

load_registry()
    ↓
parse records
    ↓
validate
    ↓
conflicts = ()

Your detect_algorithm_conflicts() exists, but the loader isn't actually using it. Also, StandardKnowledge.supersedes exists, but there isn't yet a proper semantic validation layer around it.

We're going to implement:

Sources
   │
   ├── authority rank
   └── provenance
          ↓
Algorithms / Standards
          ↓
Conflict detection
          ↓
Conservative conflict policy
          ↓
Supersession validation
Pass 2 goals

Authority ranking metadata

Deterministic authority ordering

Actual conflict detection during registry load

Conflict records persisted in KnowledgeRegistry

Validate referenced superseded standards

Prevent invalid self-supersession

Validate relationship references

Tests for all of the above

One important design decision: authority ranking will be a registry policy, not a claim that "NIST is universally better than IETF." We need deterministic behavior inside ECDAT without pretending cryptographic authority is one-dimensional.

    for source in registry.provenance:
        if source.source_type not in {
            "standard",
            "specification",
            "official_documentation",
            "research",
            "vendor",
            "secondary",
            "unknown",
        }:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Unsupported provenance source type: {source.source_type}"
            )

        if source.authority_rank < 0:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Authority rank cannot be negative: {source.source_id}"
            )

    algorithm_ids = {
        item.knowledge_id
        for item in registry.algorithms
    }
'''

if needle in text:
    text = text.replace(needle, replacement, 1)

# Ensure all referenced source IDs exist.
needle = '''    standard_ids = {
        item.standard_id
        for item in registry.standards
    }
'''

replacement = '''    for algorithm in registry.algorithms:
        missing = set(algorithm.source_ids) - source_ids
        if missing:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Algorithm {algorithm.name} references unknown sources: "
                f"{sorted(missing)}"
            )

    for standard in registry.standards:
        missing = set(standard.source_ids) - source_ids
        if missing:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Standard {standard.standard_id} references unknown sources: "
                f"{sorted(missing)}"
            )

    standard_ids = {
        item.standard_id
        for item in registry.standards
    }
'''

if needle in text:
    text = text.replace(needle, replacement, 1)

# Validate supersession references after standard IDs exist.
needle = '''    compatibility_ids = {
        item.compatibility_id
        for item in registry.compatibility
    }
'''

replacement = '''    for standard in registry.standards:
        if standard.standard_id in standard.supersedes:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Standard cannot supersede itself: {standard.standard_id}"
            )

        missing = (
            set(standard.supersedes)
            - standard_ids
        )

        if missing:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Standard {standard.standard_id} references unknown "
                f"superseded standards: {sorted(missing)}"
            )

    compatibility_ids = {
        item.compatibility_id
        for item in registry.compatibility
    }
'''

if needle in text:
    text = text.replace(needle, replacement, 1)

p.write_text(text)
PY

printf '\n===== UPDATE SOURCE AUTHORITY METADATA =====\n'

python - <<'PY'
import json
from pathlib import Path

p = Path("backend/knowledge/data/registry.json")
data = json.loads(p.read_text())

rank_map = {
    "NIST": 100,
    "IETF": 100,
}

for source in data.get("sources", []):
    authority = str(source.get("authority", "")).upper()

    source["authority_rank"] = rank_map.get(
        authority,
        source.get("authority_rank", 50),
    )

p.write_text(
    json.dumps(
        data,
        indent=2,
        ensure_ascii=True,
    ) + "\n"
)
PY

Pass 3 direction

Your current architecture is ready for this:

Artifact
   ↓
Knowledge Resolution
   ↓
Migration Relationships
   ↓
Compatibility
   ↓
Protocol Applicability
   ↓
Standards / Lifecycle / Quantum posture
   ↓
Candidate Scoring
   ↓
Evidence-backed explanation
   ↓
Recommendation
   ↓
Knowledge snapshot

The key output becomes:

JSON
{
  "target_algorithm": "X25519MLKEM768",
  "score": 94,
  "decision": "preferred",
  "reasons": [
    "hybrid migration relationship",
    "TLS 1.3 applicable",
    "protocol marked recommended",
    "quantum-resistant target",
    "high-confidence knowledge"
  ],
  "constraints": [],
  "knowledge_version": "0.5.0",
  "knowledge_hash": "..."
}

And a weaker candidate isn't simply hidden. It gets an explicit penalty:

JSON
{
  "target_algorithm": "SecP256r1MLKEM768",
  "score": 78,
  "decision": "alternative",
  "reasons": [
    "TLS 1.3 applicable",
    "hybrid migration relationship"
  ],
  "penalties": [
    "protocol recommendation flag is negative"
  ]
}

That's much more defensible than “ECDH → ML-KEM because I said so.”
